#### Security and Privacy

Conversation sharing gains public text links, and scheduled commands and messages get permission checks closer to the moment they begin. Private-session cleanup, credential masking and input handling also receive focused fixes.




##### Trusted-proxy access


Returning users behind a trusted login proxy can reopen [chat links](https://docs.openclaw.ai/web/urls) directly in the signed-in app without a public-reader or login flash once their browser has picked up the updated service worker. Each entry still checks the enrolled identity and current conversation access. First visits and token or password sign-in keep their existing handoff.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Bug fixes**

- Open chat deep links directly in the protected app for returning trusted-proxy browsers, with the public reader retained when access is denied or cannot be checked. [#168007](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168007) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).






##### Permissions, approvals, and cancellation


Scheduled commands keep their approval tied to the run through process startup, while scheduled sends through guarded HTTP, Discord, Slack and Telegram recheck permission at delivery handoff. Requests that have already started may finish. Before changing local command policy with `exec-policy set` or `preset`, stop the Gateway through its service owner; a policy change refused during pending command startup needs a later retry. See [command approvals](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/exec-approvals).

Agents can use optional [send-only access](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-tools/sessions-and-subagents#per-agent-send-only-access) to ask approved peers for help without reading their conversation history. New helpers created for restricted senders stay hidden within the same agent, workspace and permissions. Replace older helpers to apply the new inherited restriction. Affected CLI setups need a supported backend with exact OpenClaw MCP tool selection or an API runtime, with saved node execution returned to Gateway/local.

Operators can also opt into a required worker profile, which blocks unsupported local fallback and turns whose worker is unavailable. Plugin authors should check the [tool-policy migration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/hooks/tool-policy) if their hooks rewrite arguments after trusted inline approval; changed arguments now need separate approval or the call is blocked.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Approvals and current permissions**

- Require separate approval when a plugin hook changes arguments after trusted inline approval. Transforming hooks must return `requireApproval` with their final parameters. [#142700](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/142700) Thanks to [giodl73-repo](https://github.com/giodl73-repo) and [giodl73](https://github.com/giodl73).
- Keep later channel messages under their own current permissions after the temporary authority used to start the channel account ends. [#159396](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159396) Thanks to [ceckert](https://github.com/ceckert), [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa), [slinkoff](https://github.com/slinkoff) and [fuller-stack-dev](https://github.com/fuller-stack-dev).
- Keep turns waiting for plugin approval alive for the existing approval window instead of treating them as stalled early. [#161821](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161821) Thanks to [fransqaas](https://github.com/fransqaas) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Preserve the prepared command's session identity so approved remote-node commands do not fail matching because session details were omitted. [#161980](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161980) Thanks to [Program-123](https://github.com/Program-123), [steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [gaojian125](https://github.com/gaojian125), [enderwillsaveyou](https://github.com/enderwillsaveyou), [rozenpl](https://github.com/rozenpl) and [daddydubzzz](https://github.com/daddydubzzz).
- Recheck the current session before applying an answer to an agent's question or its cancellation, including when another session has replaced it with equivalent settings. [#165552](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165552) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Keep MCP app and connection buttons tied to the existing reply's delivery permission, rejecting a replaced session writer. [#165760](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165760) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Refuse delayed sharing-token requests after permission or grant changes, and recheck the conversation source before changing its history. [#167504](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167504) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Refuse reply-permission checks while the relevant database operation is unfinished. [#168029](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168029) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Scheduled commands and delivery**

- Keep scheduled-command approval valid through native process startup, refusing expired, revoked or stale authority and avoiding replay after uncertain results. Local policy edits require the running Gateway to stop first. [#164864](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164864) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Recheck scheduled sends and retries at the final handoff for guarded HTTP, Discord, Slack and Telegram, and refuse protected read results or internal replies after permission ends. Already-started requests may finish. [#164906](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164906) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve accepted parts of a scheduled message when a later part fails, with current permission checked at transport handoff. [#165129](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165129) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Agent and worker restrictions**

- Allow approved peer requests and replies without granting transcript-reading access through optional send-only permissions. A permitted peer can still include information in its reply. [#164444](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164444) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [prasad-yashdeep](https://github.com/prasad-yashdeep) and [cipp-ashe](https://github.com/cipp-ashe).
- Restrict newly created helpers for sender-limited turns to hidden sessions in the same agent and workspace, inheriting their tool and filesystem limits. Unsupported ACP and CLI paths refuse the request. [#165418](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165418) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Add opt-in `cloudWorkers.requiredProfile` enforcement before model credential use and inference. Unsupported or sessionless paths refuse local fallback, and an unavailable worker blocks the turn while allowing Gateway startup. [#166611](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166611) Thanks to [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Enforce a required worker destination during execution and recovery while preserving Stop and accepted workspace results. Keeping a local checkout does not authorize local execution. [#166613](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166613) Thanks to [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Prepare session-owned workspaces for required workers and preserve the recorded worker profile and device during recovery. [#166616](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166616) Thanks to [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa), with acknowledgment to [shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd) for related prerequisite repair work.

**Safeguards and operator controls**

- Stop a turn with recovery guidance after three consecutive identical tool failures, even when loop detection is disabled. Already-started parallel calls can exceed that threshold, and alternating failures do not trigger this rule. [#167628](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167628) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Load selected `AGENTS.md` rule sections from the effective workspace during safeguard compaction even when OpenClaw starts elsewhere. `postCompactionSections` remains opt-in. [#168446](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168446) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [jakeaadams](https://github.com/jakeaadams).
- Keep completed-send audit records for destinations affected by fallback conversation classification. [#137763](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/137763) Thanks to [teddytennant](https://github.com/teddytennant) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Cap oversized policy-watch intervals at 2,147,000,000 milliseconds so timer overflow does not cause rapid repeated checks. [#164850](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164850) Thanks to [KrasimirKralev](https://github.com/KrasimirKralev) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Avoid false secret-failure and reload warnings while restart-time database inspection is still pending; credentials remain withheld until access is admitted. [#165485](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165485) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Delete an agent from an included roster without a phantom root-file change causing refusal. Genuine changes spanning both root and included files still require separate handling. [#166316](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166316) Thanks to [jayzhou2309](https://github.com/jayzhou2309), [jaoa12or](https://github.com/jaoa12or) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).






##### Private sessions, secrets, and source trust


Published conversations can use their normal chat URL for [read-only public text access](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/multi-user#world-readable-session-links). Publication includes existing and future user messages and final assistant replies, while excluding tools, reasoning and attachments. Review the conversation before publishing. Credential redaction is best effort, and turning off public access cannot recall copies someone already downloaded. Eligible X work sessions can opt into publication; this is off by default and restricted to admitted maintainer interactions with a verified public source.

Self-hosted login-proxy deployments need the [public-link handler and proxy policy](https://docs.openclaw.ai/web/urls#login-proxy-deployment) before enabling anonymous reading. Direct Incognito links can still show unavailable or return 403 to signed-in administrators. Resetting an Incognito session now cleans up its managed checkout when safe, but unsaved-work snapshots and arbitrary files written by tools remain on disk.

The [secret egress proxy](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/secrets/secret-store-and-egress) also generates certificates compatible with strict TLS clients. Restart the Gateway after updating, then start new commands to pick up the fresh trust bundle. Retained legacy debug-proxy certificates are preserved and may still fail strict verification.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Conversation links and Incognito**

- Publish read-only conversation text at normal chat URLs, retain revocation for old token links, and add optional publication for eligible X work sessions. Re-enabling publication restores the same canonical URL. [#166049](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166049) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete) for request and design collaboration.
- Let public chat links reach normal session lookup and login after startup completes instead of incorrectly reporting temporary unavailability. [#167604](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167604) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Restore authorized Incognito message sending that could fail with `SESSION_MUTATION_AUTHORIZATION_CHANGED`, while retaining current permission checks. [a5a43a0](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/a5a43a0362a23c123232367a75a8a34606bd4710)
- Clean up the managed checkout and branch on Incognito reset, saving unsaved work in a snapshot or reporting why the checkout was preserved. [#165185](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165185) Thanks to [jayzhou2309](https://github.com/jayzhou2309), [steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Prepare account labels and shared-session attribution with less synchronous database work, and provide prepared-identity helpers for plugins. [#167520](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167520) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Credentials and secret handling**

- Mask reflected credentials in Discord PluralKit lookup-failure diagnostics while keeping useful error context. [#141317](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/141317) Thanks to [hugenshen](https://github.com/hugenshen) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Mask built-in credential patterns across long-output chunk boundaries and fix related redaction stalls and stack-overflow crashes. Custom patterns retain their bounded scanning behavior. [#161480](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161480) Thanks to [eleqtrizit](https://github.com/eleqtrizit).
- Reduce CPU work when masking large text and structured content while preserving existing masking behavior. [#166701](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166701) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Mask recognizable reflected tokens and session cookies in LINE and Tlon failure diagnostics while retaining error text and HTTP status. [#167234](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167234) Thanks to [xialonglee](https://github.com/xialonglee) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Generate secret-proxy certificates with the identifiers strict TLS clients require. [#165818](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165818) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [vortexopenclaw](https://github.com/vortexopenclaw) and [RyanSandoval](https://github.com/RyanSandoval), with investigation acknowledgments to [A1fred-AI](https://github.com/A1fred-AI), [gisk0](https://github.com/gisk0) and [jfk-hue](https://github.com/jfk-hue).
- Reject POSIX named pipes passed as secret-file paths instead of hanging; use stdin for [streaming secret input](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/secrets). The same change keeps asynchronous plugin diagnostics out of stdout, prevents Doctor from deleting replaced cleanup targets, and preserves custom Mistral costs for special model IDs. [#165900](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165900) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Update MCP dependencies for OAuth advisory [GHSA-6qxp-vccf-f47h](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6qxp-vccf-f47h), and preserve the original client's credentials when background refresh discovers a different issuer. Switching issuer requires explicit login. [#166217](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166217) Thanks to [Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen), [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc) and [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Clarify [which tasks can consume protected secrets](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/secrets) before an agent asks you to enter one. Protected SSH, sudo and arbitrary shell-template use are unsupported; explicit environment secrets remain agent-readable plaintext on the Gateway host. [#167976](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167976) Thanks to [romneyda](https://github.com/romneyda) and [markma84](https://github.com/markma84).

**Reply content and source trust**

- Keep unfinished hidden reasoning out of Chat Completions replies when a native tool call is followed by a token limit or content filter. [#168294](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168294) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve untrusted network-source labels through Tool Search and Code Mode results and subsequent replies, maintaining the existing [memory trust boundary](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture#trust-boundaries-and-limits) for new messages. Historical transcripts and memory are not rewritten. [#169560](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/169560) Thanks to [RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa) and [farisoftfry](https://github.com/farisoftfry).






##### Text and command input checks


Text imports now reject malformed UTF-8 in affected scheduling, backup, plugin-authoring, connection and browser commands instead of silently substituting characters. Valid Unicode remains supported. Save the affected input as valid UTF-8 before retrying; for `oc-path` edits, repair a backed-up copy first. These checks cannot restore values altered by an earlier command.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Input validation**

- Reject an explicitly blank camera `--facing` value before contacting a device. Omit the option when no facing preference is intended. [#145538](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/145538) Thanks to [hugenshen](https://github.com/hugenshen) and [altaywtf](https://github.com/altaywtf).
- Reject malformed UTF-8 in [cron](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/cron) script and private-note imports before writing altered text. [#167120](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167120) Thanks to [Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Refuse backup verification when manifest bytes are malformed UTF-8, leaving the archive unchanged. [#167389](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167389) Thanks to [Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Reject malformed UTF-8 during plugin build and validation before rewriting either author-owned metadata file. [#167391](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167391) Thanks to [Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Preserve malformed UTF-8 [connect target files](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/connect) byte for byte and return a clear error. Valid text can still be consumed if later pairing fails. [#167399](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167399) Thanks to [Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Refuse malformed UTF-8 files or stdin in [browser form and batch commands](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/browser) before changing page values. [#167412](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167412) Thanks to [Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Make opt-in `oc-path` edits and dry runs refuse invalid UTF-8 with `OC_PATH_INPUT_NOT_UTF8` and exit code 2, preserving unrelated file bytes. [#168491](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168491) Thanks to [wangmiao0668000666](https://github.com/wangmiao0668000666) and [yetval](https://github.com/yetval).






##### Dependency security updates


The transitive CSS and build-tool dependency `source-map-js` moves from 1.2.1 to patched 1.2.2, addressing pathological source-map processing covered by [GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q).



###### Sources and complete change list


**Dependency updates**

- Select patched `source-map-js` 1.2.2 for CSS-tree, Magicast and PostCSS consumers. [#165902](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165902) Thanks to [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).







#### Quality-of-Life Improvements




##### Keep conversations and queued work moving


Follow-up messages keep their place while a reply finishes, preparation completes or an eligible conversation recovers from a Gateway restart. Eligible background command results can return to the chat that requested them without waiting for a periodic heartbeat, under the existing routing and quiet settings. [Restart recovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/restart-recovery) preserves the original user's valid permissions, and cancelled work stays cancelled.

When receipt of a follow-up cannot be confirmed, OpenClaw leaves running tools alone and explains the uncertainty. A lost connection can also leave work running, so check conversation history and command effects before retrying. After sleep or a backward clock change, the Gateway must still confirm that it owns the original stored data.

**Restarts and interrupted replies**

##### Follow delegated work back to its parent


Delegated results return to their requesting parent through queued follow-ups and eligible restart recovery. Interrupted native child work can return privately to the original parent with retained history, even when cleanup had been requested. Normal history-retention limits still apply. Cancelled or reset work stays cancelled, and the parent must check uncertain command effects before retrying. See [delegated result delivery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/subagents/announce).

##### Run and stop commands


Browser Stop and `/stop` end the request's ordinary commands, wait for cleanup and preserve their output without sending a late completion reply. This also covers selected native child and descendant commands in the running Gateway, but command tracking does not survive a Gateway restart. Cleanup uncertainty is reported. Explicit background services keep their own lifetime and must be stopped through their handles, as explained in [background processes](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/background-process).

Commands on paired Windows nodes preserve quoted prompts, URLs and paths with spaces. Linux nodes keep tools from their service `PATH` after login profiles run, and execution-policy failures with `--json` produce a structured error and an unsuccessful exit status.

##### Manage agents and settings


Successful agent creation now waits until the running Gateway can use the agent, and successful updates or deletions wait for the Gateway to apply them. If a management request returns `UNAVAILABLE`, the change may already be saved. Inspect it with `config.get` before retrying. [Configuration guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/config) also gives clearer advice when a proposed update is rejected or a value is unset.

Partial agent creation keeps the agent and successful bindings but exits unsuccessfully so you can resolve the remaining conflicts. Deleting an agent still leaves retained bot accounts to retire separately; follow the [agent routing guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/multi-agent#routing-rules) before rebinding them.

##### CLI commands and completion


Fish completions preserve literal backslashes, and the macOS CLI handles invalid ports and unusually large discovery timeouts without numeric-conversion crashes. Concurrent [Gateway CLI calls](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/gateway/query) reuse credential-loading work, while CLI-backed conversations can account for a preceding history update before starting their next reply.

##### Useful errors and diagnostics


Provider, startup and delivery failures give more useful causes and recovery guidance, including when a queued CLI send is confirmed not to have been delivered. Channel status shows recorded health without waiting on live providers; use an explicit `--probe` for a fresh check. See [channel commands](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/channels) and [Gateway diagnostics](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/diagnostics).

Diagnostic-history cleanup can continue while new events arrive and holds the database writer for shorter periods, though the overall cleanup may take longer. The first writable open after the trajectory-history index change needs temporary disk space. Keep a complete matching backup before upgrading stored data; [database history](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/database-schemas/storage-changes) explains changes that a package downgrade alone cannot undo.

##### Less repeated work during everyday use


Session searches, file lists and shared conversation catalogs avoid repeated history reads, while image-heavy chats can reuse prepared images between turns. Concurrent viewers can share list preparation while keeping their own permissions. Background work also avoids several unnecessary database checks and copies.

New tool-result saves use a format that older runtimes cannot replay. Keep a complete pre-upgrade backup before using it. Worker limits also trade duplicated memory against waiting time, and some background processing uses extra CPU or memory. [Stored-data changes](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/database-schemas/storage-changes) and [session pruning](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/session-pruning) explain the relevant upgrade and history behavior.

##### Self-hosted workers and remote sessions


Operators can opt selected paired workers into [worker-local model requests](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/native-inference) with `settings.inference=worker`. The Gateway still authorizes turns and keeps history, while the worker uses its configured model endpoint and credentials. Compatible Gateway, node and worker builds are required; Windows and nested containers are unsupported. Worker code retains its local user's access to files and credentials. Self-hosted [Agents API executors](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/agentsapi#self-hosted-execution) also require operator-supplied hosting, tools and credential integration.

Experimental `openclaw fleet` commands have been removed. Existing containers, networks, data and registry entries remain, and their images are not updated. Inventory and back up these deployments, then manage them with Docker or Podman lifecycle tools. Follow the [retirement guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install/updating#retired-tenant-container-management) and use a complete matching backup and build for [downgrade recovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install/updating/rollback-and-recovery#downgrade).

##### Git workspaces and recovery


Managed Git workspaces can prepare concurrently, reuse branch discovery and recover recognized missing-object failures when upstream objects are available. Archiving saves the session's archive state before background checkout cleanup finishes; unarchive needs the repository and any required saved snapshot. If fetching a default branch fails, new automatic worktrees may use the last fetched default, which can be old. Local-only repositories need an explicit base such as `HEAD`. See [managed worktrees](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/managed-worktrees).

**Review worktree capacity before upgrading or lowering its limit.** The global `worktreeMaxCount` defaults to 4096. Capacity eviction can delete manual, dirty, nested or moved-branch checkouts, and a failed best-effort snapshot does not prevent deletion. Back up unsaved work and read [capacity and eviction](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/managed-worktrees#capacity-and-eviction). Cleanup now returns a background job to poll for its outcome and issues; a successful request does not mean cleanup has finished. The latest cleanup receipt is lost on Gateway restart.

#### Other Bug Fixes

These fixes address interrupted conversation saves, overlapping history reads and cleanup, startup checks, and replies that finish without a visible answer.




##### State reads, writes, and cleanup


Conversation saves finish in order, and several fixes let history searches, progress updates and new messages proceed together without getting stuck waiting on one another. State reads can also resume between cleanup retries when a database resource fails to close. Other changes reduce repeated database work when opening, saving and maintaining conversations.

Agent databases move from schema 24 to 25. To roll back afterward, you need the matching pre-upgrade backup and older build, and restoring that backup discards later writes. Reinstalling the old package alone is insufficient. Follow the [database rollback guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/database-schemas/versioning#schema-bumps-and-older-updaters).



###### Sources and complete change list


**Conversation saves and concurrent access**

- A pending turn can replace a database worker that exits before receiving its operation, without replaying writes already sent. [#163258](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163258), thanks to [@goutamadwant](https://github.com/goutamadwant), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@hawkit](https://github.com/hawkit).
- A failed write iterator no longer prevents the next valid write. [c190df1](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/c190df12716a17d23a1c00eaf106044d2f2c9317).
- Conflicting conversation saves retry locally once and report a save error without repeating model work or switching accounts. [#165490](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165490), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Accepted conversation saves finish before later writes or shutdown can overtake them, and successive transcript saves cannot reuse an earlier success without storing the new event. [#165894](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165894), [#165904](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165904), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- New messages can be saved and acknowledged while unrelated history reads are blocked. [#166365](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166365), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Progress-card saves and history searches avoid a circular wait that could stall other chat writes. [#167396](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167396), thanks to [@fuller-stack-dev](https://github.com/fuller-stack-dev).
- Simultaneous first session requests avoid intermittent database initialization failures on fresh Gateways. [#167515](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167515), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Saved changes remain committed when an ordinary notification fails; incomplete cached results are discarded without repeating the write. [#167570](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167570), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- History, chat delivery, Boards and transcript loading avoid specific circular waits when reads, writes, cleanup and usage refresh overlap. [#167662](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167662), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Session readers and plugin callbacks retain committed conversation and sharing changes when a worker reply is lost, while delayed results preserve newer assignments. [#167875](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167875), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- History reads used to prepare chats and inspect replies avoid another writer-reader circular wait. [#167901](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167901), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Session writes reject invalid serialized records with less repeated validation work. This introduces schema 25 and its backup-based rollback requirement. [#167932](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167932), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chats can retry maintenance conflicts known to have rolled back, preserving the original request across model fallback. Unknown outcomes still return an error. [#168078](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168078), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@jacobtomlinson](https://github.com/jacobtomlinson).
- State reads can resume between retries after a database resource fails to clean up. Protected file replacement and native database retirement failures can still keep reads blocked. [#171709](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/171709), thanks to [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa), [@residentb2b](https://github.com/residentb2b), [@homeworld520](https://github.com/homeworld520) and [@sunny251008-debug](https://github.com/sunny251008-debug).

**Opening conversations and reading saved state**

- Database reader cleanup handles an unreadable candidate beneath a regular file without throwing. [357466e](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/357466e56837b934ef04dd7e54917c68f519ccb1).
- Newly created databases remain ready through worker handoffs, and replacement checks retire obsolete copies reached through alternate paths. [400bab4](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/400bab4a7937e6e12a742ef4bb7121d7f9b852eb).
- Completed database-opening requests can be released while their workers remain available for reuse. [4db0224](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/4db022470af693f9f78dafd2a39338d5cd7dadda).
- A closed database deleted from disk can be recreated through a directory alias. [58e1c3f](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/58e1c3f789ad14c62dd09603dd1d3d8bd3841fd1).
- Shared-database ownership records are validated even when no writes are pending. Malformed records are refused. [7cbba45](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/7cbba45c1323c89021fb50bda7c5dbaa736490ec).
- Routine database work reuses validated information while detecting schema and policy changes. [#164490](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164490), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Initial session lists, common session checks, and status, Side chat and progress reads use background database workers. [#165027](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165027), [#165028](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165028), [#165205](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165205), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Small session edits avoid waiting for history loading and return smaller receipts. Integrations no longer receive `skillsSnapshot` or `systemPromptReport` in those receipts; the saved data remains available. [#165073](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165073), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Routine session checks and reply finalization avoid loading large saved prompts and diff data they do not use. [#165181](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165181), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Routine session-start and completion saves use background workers, while session reads reuse current ownership information and existing transactions. [#165193](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165193), [#165293](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165293), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Initial saved-session replies prepare their database reads in workers across setup, slash commands and native confirmations. [#165295](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165295), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Ordinary chat startup rereads current routing and permission information through workers; permission and skill preparation also preserves the order and original destination of messages sent during active tasks. [#165360](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165360), [#165370](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165370), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Repeated conversation-search readiness checks reuse current transcript status. Custom `OpenClawAgentSqliteWorkerStore` adapters now require `execute` and `executeExisting`; adapters built around `run` and `close` may need changes. [#165377](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165377), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- First-turn timestamp recovery and agent diagnostic setup move repeated database reads into workers. [#165401](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165401), [#165410](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165410), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Sessions in skipped stores report that they cannot be found instead of repeatedly attempting an ineffective reload. [#165408](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165408), thanks to [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus) and [@Oldrich333](https://github.com/Oldrich333).
- Message and usage views prepare targeted database reads in workers; saved-chat model and account metadata follows the same path. [#165470](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165470), [#165614](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165614), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), with [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd) acknowledged for prior type-import work.
- Session validity checks avoid loading large saved prompts and move eligible delegated-agent skill checks into workers. [#165495](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165495), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Corrections to saved replies and media perform transcript work in background workers while preserving unrelated history. [#165541](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165541), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Linux Gateways periodically warm a bounded amount of recent database data to reduce disk reads when opening recent history. [#165545](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165545), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Sharing checks use session workers, with queued messages checked against current permissions before dispatch and saving. [#165600](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165600), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- First-use database checks use existing workers, and independent observer saves queue without blocking turn cleanup. [#165680](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165680), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Recently used database workers remain available when switching between agents. Up to four idle workers are retained for 30 minutes, using roughly 110 MiB per additional worker in the recorded measurement. [#165993](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165993), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Profile, recent-project and session views prepare profile data in workers and avoid repeated lookups. [#166283](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166283), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Targeted history, Files-panel and pre-reset memory reads use existing workers. [#166293](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166293), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Repeated session, history and search work reuses readers and completed database checks, while chat turns reuse newly saved session details. [#166503](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166503), [#166519](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166519), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@hippoley](https://github.com/hippoley) for #166503.
- Creating sessions in large catalogs avoids repeated full inventory scans when readers reopen. [#166541](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166541), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Eligible local fresh-session first messages use fewer commits during concurrent creation. A crash between creating the session and committing its first input can leave an empty session; the run is acknowledged only after the input is committed. See [session lifecycle](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/session). [#166891](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166891), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Ordinary saved input avoids a redundant history reload before the next model turn. [#167682](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167682), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Android and Termux recognize the same healthy database after ordinary writes instead of reporting a false database replacement. [#167719](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167719), thanks to [@yxshee](https://github.com/yxshee), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@grabowskidaniel359-design](https://github.com/grabowskidaniel359-design).

**Reduced repeated database work**

- Routine history checks and metadata saves use fewer statements, chat and recovery reads batch related information, and unchanged conversation and participant details can be reused. [#166041](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166041), [#166053](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166053), [#166054](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166054), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat and history paths reduce routine bookkeeping and repeated database-object preparation. [#166074](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166074), [#166102](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166102), [#166253](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166253), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Conversation discovery batches database work and saves session associations in workers. [#166259](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166259), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat-turn bookkeeping and startup layout checks repeat less work, with interruption cleanup kept on the originally accepted conversation. [#166674](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166674), [#166698](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166698), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat preparation repeats fewer session lookups and follows acknowledged compaction when reading saved history. [#166703](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166703), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Preparing chats reuses fresh history and delivery information; chat and background maintenance also avoid redundant checks. [#166804](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166804), [#166966](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166966), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Fresh database workers and maintenance reuse the expected database layout already prepared by the Gateway. [#167001](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167001), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat turns reuse history snapshots and delivery information. [#167238](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167238), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat preparation repeats less setup, while accepted memory indexing keeps its database executor available through provider waits and cleanup. [#167263](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167263), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), with [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd) acknowledged for earlier fixture cleanup.
- Chat saves and maintenance reuse validated information and combine nearby updates; turn finalization avoids redundant preparation while preserving pending model choices for their own turn. [#167291](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167291), [#167685](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167685), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Chat preparation reuses current session information, and saves avoid redundant reads and nested database savepoints. [#167686](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167686), [#167690](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167690), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Cleanup, shutdown, and integrity checks**

- Settled databases can close while unrelated shutdown cleanup is pending, including busy restarts where plugin cleanup stalls. [#164788](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164788), [#165069](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165069), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Eligible native Linux crash restarts can open sessions while a full integrity scan continues in the background; large session repairs also avoid repeated cleanup work. [#164846](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164846), [#165070](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165070), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Automatic session cleanup reads delegated-agent protections in workers, preserving sessions still active or awaiting delivery. [#164851](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164851), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), with [@todddickerson](https://github.com/todddickerson) acknowledged for related reporting.
- Doctor imports sessions blocked by older migration receipts while preserving existing database metadata and retained recovery files where applicable. See [SQLite maintenance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/doctor/sqlite-maintenance). [#164867](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164867), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@hshs38033-cmd](https://github.com/hshs38033-cmd).
- Plugin cleanup reads installation records in workers; session-cleanup plans load only matching snapshots and later skip recent sessions while scanning old transcript inventories in smaller transactions. [#165029](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165029), [#165082](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165082), [#165254](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165254), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Transcript status and orphaned search-index cleanup avoid repeating ownership checks for every event. [#165090](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165090), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Shared-state connections reuse valid completed integrity checks, and slow-operation warnings identify time spent waiting or committing. [#165186](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165186), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Session cleanup repeats less discovery work on large delegated-agent registries. [#165511](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165511), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Supervised external restarts can hand over after required saves when unrelated plugin shutdown stalls; interrupted restarts also cancel scheduled continuations before closing databases. [#165524](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165524), [#165782](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165782), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Failed session initialization can finish cleanup when permission checks need synchronous database access. [#165859](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165859), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Idle Gateways avoid premature scheduler wakeups and unnecessary temporary promise joins. [#165931](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165931), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Process-owned stops that exhaust their active-work wait can still settle accepted writes and close databases cleanly. Required persistence can continue to delay exit. [#166018](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166018), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Background integrity checks keep their database worker available until verification finishes. [978478f](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/978478ff01afcb73143bc63da5f19f1218c888b7). Completed checks survive eligible orderly closes and idle worker retirement, avoiding redundant full scans. [#166112](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166112), [#166412](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166412), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- A successful background check completed during startup can be saved for the next clean restart. [#166972](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166972), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Maintenance and first session-list reads reduce repeated processing of large delegated-agent histories. [#167010](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167010), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Manual cleanup and history trimming use background workers. Session reset and cleanup also avoid unnecessary refreshes when protected history remains intact. [#167070](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167070), [#167549](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167549), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), with [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen) acknowledged for historical repair work in #167549.
- An isolated database awaiting preparation no longer prevents the Gateway from accepting connections. The affected store stays unavailable until its checks pass; live legacy ACP bindings still require offline Doctor repair. [#167627](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167627), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Eligible current-schema native WAL databases can open while a missing integrity receipt is checked in the background. Damage discovered afterward can still stop access and quarantine affected work. See [integrity checks](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/database-schemas/integrity-and-recovery). [#168008](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168008), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Background session cleanup holds the database writer for less time when conversation creation and archival overlap. [#168013](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168013), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Doctor and update recovery can pause background checkpoints while retaining workers for stable recovery captures. Expected checkpoint deferral during another process's maintenance no longer appears as a failure. [#170022](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/170022), [#170783](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/170783), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).






##### Startup and readiness


An agent still preparing its database can remain temporarily unavailable while ready agents and the dashboard continue working. Replies can wait for their own agent to finish preparing, and a specific startup dependency loop no longer leaves worker recovery stuck after the server has opened its listener. Startup commands and diagnostics also distinguish a running process from one ready to serve requests.

A known limitation remains: an agent's child conversations can stay hidden when saved in a database outside the current agent configuration, even after the agent is re-registered.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Startup fixes**

- Failed starts show the original cause before any cleanup failure. [#164500](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164500), thanks to [@sunlit-deng](https://github.com/sunlit-deng), [@waynegault](https://github.com/waynegault) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Delayed chat startup retains the current caller and conversation, and agents sharing a global key retain separate histories, attachments and replies. [#164682](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164682), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@galiniliev](https://github.com/galiniliev).
- Leftover unused databases no longer block readiness in the covered case, with their files preserved for Doctor inspection. A known limitation remains where a configured agent's child conversations can be hidden in an unconfigured store. [#164929](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164929), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@Oldrich333](https://github.com/Oldrich333).
- Agents awaiting database-index repair report retryable unavailability while health checks, the dashboard and admitted agents remain usable. Physical database corruption still needs explicit Doctor repair. [#165323](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165323), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Accepted chat messages are acknowledged while skill preparation continues; preparation failures appear on the run, and acknowledgment does not mean execution has finished. [#165608](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165608), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Startup reuses configuration checks and can defer slower database preparation while other services start. [#165667](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165667), [#165803](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165803), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Startup waits up to five minutes through temporary database ownership contention. Actual offline maintenance still refuses access immediately. [#166111](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166111), thanks to [@NianJiuZst](https://github.com/NianJiuZst), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@ericcaiwx-star](https://github.com/ericcaiwx-star) and [@mashaohua1999-stack](https://github.com/mashaohua1999-stack).
- Restart readiness inspects shared database state in a child process while preserving refusal checks. [#166252](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166252), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- `gateway start` waits for an existing process to become ready without restarting it. Scripts should handle exit 1 for readiness failure and exit 2 when it is still starting; the process remains running. See [Gateway service commands](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/gateway/service). [#166942](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166942), thanks to [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa) and [@odrobnik](https://github.com/odrobnik).
- Status and Doctor retain meaningful startup errors after cleanup messages. Reported log errors can come from an earlier run. [#166946](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166946), thanks to [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa) and [@odrobnik](https://github.com/odrobnik).
- Session and model reads after restart can wait up to 20 seconds for the selected agent's database, then return a retryable failure if it is still unavailable. [#167674](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167674), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Healthy starts avoid misleading lock warnings for nonblocking attempts already managed by a retry. [#167754](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167754), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- New replies wait for their own agent's initial readiness while ready agents continue serving. This wait can be cancelled. [#168826](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/168826), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@xiaozishan](https://github.com/xiaozishan).
- Deferred database preparation can finish before worker recovery needs it, breaking a startup dependency cycle. [#170348](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/170348), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).






##### Streamed replies


Empty keepalives from OpenAI-compatible streams no longer keep a stalled reply alive indefinitely, and a required answer that remains empty after retries shows a saved try-again notice. Strict Chat Completions providers can also continue conversations containing runtime notices, while long explanations with quoted tool-call examples avoid repeated parsing that could delay delivery.



###### Sources and complete change list


**Reply fixes**

- Empty OpenAI-compatible keepalives no longer defeat progress timeouts, allowing normal retry and configured fallback. Hidden reasoning still counts as progress; endpoints that legitimately send only empty events for long periods may need their existing timeout adjusted. See the [agent loop](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/agent-loop). [#165983](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165983), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@Irish-Joseph](https://github.com/Irish-Joseph), [@cestercian](https://github.com/cestercian), [@rmyers64](https://github.com/rmyers64) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Strict [Chat Completions providers](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/model-providers/custom-providers) receive runtime notices in a supported role, so existing chats can continue without clearing history. [#167309](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167309), thanks to [@jlapenna](https://github.com/jlapenna) and [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Required turns that exhaust retries with empty, invisible or reasoning-only output show and save the try-again error instead of appearing cancelled with no answer. [#167972](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/167972), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Long streamed explanations following quoted tool-call XML in Markdown code blocks avoid repeatedly parsing the whole answer. Examples remain visible and payload filtering remains active. [#171573](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/171573), thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@boeingchoco](https://github.com/boeingchoco).
